Configuration

the options that govern all of this

The configuration schema is the binary’s own

This is read straight out of the shipped binary, from the JSON Schema it emits for zeroclaw config schema — the same document the gateway serves at GET /api/config. Every property below comes out of it; nothing is transcribed by hand.

The configuration schema declares 89 sections in total. The 19 that govern the tool surface are set out in full below — 310 properties between them, each with its type, its default and its description.

sectionproperties shownwhat it governs
tools1Which tools exist, and which are optional.
shell_tool1How the shell tool is constrained and supervised.
browser18Backends, and the domains the browser may reach.
cron12How scheduled jobs are delivered.
delegate2How the delegate tool spawns and scopes subagents.
hooks17Declarative hooks that run around the agent.
skills25User-defined capabilities and their creation policy.
skill_bundles3Bundled skill sets.
plugins26Plugin processes and the capabilities they hold.
mcp14Connected MCP servers and the tools they expose.
mcp_bundles2Deferred MCP servers, loaded on demand.
memory63Long-term memory policy.
sop25SOP execution and approval gates.
permission_profiles5What each profile allows.
risk_profiles20The risk tiers the permission profiles grant.
agents35Per-agent overrides, including the tools each may use.
security29The security posture the runtime enforces.
reliability7Retries, timeouts and circuit breaking.
trust5What the agent is allowed to trust from outside.

Builtin tools

Which tools exist, and which are optional.

Optional model-visible built-ins (`[tools]`). The default is full; `optional = []` selects the eleven core tools. Selection never grants execution permission. Reload after edits.

propertytype / defaultdescription
tools.optionalarraydefault ["*"]
Built-in selection: `"*"`, the default, is full (every compiled-in built-in permitted by its config and policy); empty is minimal (eleven core tools). Named entries add tools to minimal and are redundant alongside `"*"`.

Shell tool

How the shell tool is constrained and supervised.

Shell tool configuration (`[shell_tool]`).

propertytype / defaultdescription
shell_tool.timeout_secsintegerdefault 60
Maximum shell command execution time in seconds (default: 60).

Browser

Backends, and the domains the browser may reach.

Browser automation configuration (`[browser]`).

propertytype / defaultdescription
browser.allowed_domainsarraydefault ["*"]
Allowed domains for `browser_open` (exact or subdomain match)
browser.allowed_private_hostsarraydefault []
Private/internal hosts allowed to bypass SSRF protection. Exact and subdomain matches are supported; `["*"]` permits **all** private/local hosts (RFC 1918, loopback, link-local, `.local`). Default: empty (deny). Listed hosts also bypass `allowed_domains`. Both the `browser` tool and `browser_open` accept `http://` for listed hosts — internal services frequently lack a public TLS cert. Warning: `["*"]` also reaches link-local addresses, including the cloud metadata endpoint (`169.254.169.254`) — list specific hosts unless you accept that exposure.
browser.automation_enabledbooleandefault false
Enable the full `browser` automation tool (Chrome/Chromium control: navigate, click, type, read page content). Opt-in and independent of `enabled`, which gates only `browser_open`. Automation acts inside browser sessions that may already be logged in, so on an always-on agent a prompt-injected message could drive them; leave this off unless the agent needs it. It is also absent from [`default_auto_approve`], so `browser` calls hit the approval gate unless a risk profile lists it explicitly.
browser.backendstringdefault "agent_browser"
Browser automation backend: "agent_browser" | "rust_native" | "computer_use" | "auto"
browser.computer_useobject
Computer-use sidecar configuration (`[browser.computer_use]` section). Delegates OS-level mouse, keyboard, and screenshot actions to a local sidecar.
browser.computer_use.allow_remote_endpointbooleandefault false
Allow remote/public endpoint for computer-use sidecar (default: false)
browser.computer_use.api_keystring / null
Optional bearer token for computer-use sidecar
browser.computer_use.endpointstringdefault "http://127.0.0.1:8787/v1/actions"
Sidecar endpoint for computer-use actions (OS-level mouse/keyboard/screenshot)
browser.computer_use.max_coordinate_xinteger / null
Optional X-axis boundary for coordinate-based actions
browser.computer_use.max_coordinate_yinteger / null
Optional Y-axis boundary for coordinate-based actions
browser.computer_use.timeout_msintegerdefault 15000
Per-action request timeout in milliseconds
browser.computer_use.window_allowlistarraydefault []
Optional window title/process allowlist forwarded to sidecar policy
browser.enabledbooleandefault true
Enable `browser_open` tool (opens URLs in the system browser without scraping)
browser.headedboolean / null
Show browser window for agent_browser backend. When unset, inherits AGENT_BROWSER_HEADED.
browser.native_chrome_pathstring / null
Optional Chrome/Chromium executable path for rust-native backend
browser.native_headlessbooleandefault true
Headless mode for rust-native backend
browser.native_webdriver_urlstringdefault "http://127.0.0.1:9515"
WebDriver endpoint URL for rust-native backend (e.g. `http://127.0.0.1:9515`)
browser.session_namestring / null
Browser session name (for agent-browser automation)

Cron

How scheduled jobs are delivered.

Declarative cron jobs (`[cron.<alias>]`), alias-keyed. Each entry is a named scheduled job synced into the database at scheduler startup. Subsystem runtime knobs (enable/disable, catch-up, run-history retention) live on `[scheduler]`.

A map: one entry per alias. The properties below are the shape of a single entry.

propertytype / defaultdescription
cron.<name>.allowed_toolsarray / null
Optional allowlist of tool names for agent jobs. When omitted, scheduler defaults may still exclude scheduler mutation tools for cron agent jobs.
cron.<name>.commandstring / null
Shell command to run (required when `job_type = "shell"`).
cron.<name>.deliveryanyOf
Delivery configuration.
cron.<name>.enabledbooleandefault true
Whether the job is enabled. Default: `true`.
cron.<name>.job_typestringdefault "shell"
Job type: `"shell"` (default) or `"agent"`.
cron.<name>.modelstring / null
Model override for agent jobs.
cron.<name>.namestring / null
Human-readable name.
cron.<name>.promptstring / null
Agent prompt (required when `job_type = "agent"`).
cron.<name>.scheduleoneOf
Schedule variant for declarative cron jobs.
cron.<name>.session_targetstring / null
Session target: `"isolated"` (default) or `"main"`.
cron.<name>.shell_output_formatoneOf
Output format for shell cron job stdout.
cron.<name>.uses_memorybooleandefault true
Whether to recall and inject memory context before this agent job runs. Defaults to `true`; set to `false` for stateless digest jobs.

Delegate

How the delegate tool spawns and scopes subagents.

Delegate tool global default configuration (`[delegate]`).

propertytype / defaultdescription
delegate.agentic_timeout_secsintegerdefault 300
Default timeout in seconds for agentic sub-agent runs. Can be overridden per-agent in `[agents.<name>]` config. Default: 300 seconds.
delegate.timeout_secsintegerdefault 120
Default timeout in seconds for non-agentic sub-agent model_provider calls. Can be overridden per-agent in `[agents.<name>]` config. Default: 120 seconds.

Hooks

Declarative hooks that run around the agent.

Hooks configuration (lifecycle hooks and built-in hook toggles).

propertytype / defaultdescription
hooks.builtinobject
hooks.builtin.command_loggerbooleandefault false
Enable the command-logger hook (logs tool calls for auditing).
hooks.builtin.webhook_auditobject
Configuration for the webhook-audit builtin hook. Sends an HTTP POST with a JSON body to an external endpoint each time a tool call matches one of the configured patterns. Useful for centralised audit logging, SIEM ingestion, or compliance pipelines.
hooks.builtin.webhook_audit.enabledbooleandefault false
Enable the webhook-audit hook. Default: `false`.
hooks.builtin.webhook_audit.include_argsbooleandefault false
Include tool call arguments in the audit payload. Default: `false`. Arguments are scrubbed for common credentials and recognised inline image markers before export, but may still contain unrecognised secrets or personal data. The destination controls retention of exported payloads.
hooks.builtin.webhook_audit.max_args_bytesintegerdefault 4096
Maximum source bytes retained from scrubbed, serialised arguments before the truncation marker is appended. The marker and enclosing audit-payload JSON do not count toward this limit. Arguments exceeding it are truncated on a UTF-8 boundary and exported as a string instead of their original JSON structure. Default: `4096`.
hooks.builtin.webhook_audit.tool_patternsarraydefault []
Glob patterns for tool names to audit (e.g. `["Bash", "Write"]`). An empty list means **no** tools are audited.
hooks.builtin.webhook_audit.urlstringdefault ""
Target URL that will receive the audit POST requests.
hooks.commandsarraydefault []
Declarative hook rules. Each entry maps a lifecycle event to a command: the command receives the event payload as JSON on stdin and answers with a JSON decision (`continue`, `block`, or `rewrite`) on stdout. A script is a hook — no plugin runtime and no rebuild required. ```toml [[hooks.commands]] event = "before_tool_call" matcher = "shell" command = "bin/audit-shell.sh" timeout_ms = 2000 ```
hooks.commands[]commandstring
The command to run. It receives the event payload as JSON on stdin.
hooks.commands[]envobjectdefault {}
Extra environment entries for the command.
hooks.commands[]eventoneOf
The lifecycle event a declarative hook observes.
hooks.commands[]fail_openbooleandefault true
When the command fails or times out, continue (true, the default) or block the operation (false).
hooks.commands[]matcherstring / null
Only fire for tools whose name matches this glob. Tool events only.
hooks.commands[]priorityintegerdefault 0
Ordering among declarative hooks. Higher runs first.
hooks.commands[]timeout_msinteger / null
How long the command may run before it is killed.
hooks.enabledbooleandefault true
Enable lifecycle hook execution. Hooks run in-process with the same privileges as the main runtime. Keep enabled hook handlers narrowly scoped and auditable.

Skills

User-defined capabilities and their creation policy.

Skills loading and community repository behavior (`[skills]`).

propertytype / defaultdescription
skills.allow_scriptsbooleandefault false
Allow script-like files in skills (`.sh`, `.bash`, `.ps1`, shebang shell files). Default: `false` (secure by default).
skills.extra_registriesarray
Additional user-configured skill registries, installed via `registry:<name>/<skill>`. Each reuses the git-clone registry path and is cloned to its own `extra-registry-<name>/` workspace directory.
skills.extra_registries[]enabledbooleandefault true
Whether this registry is eligible for installs. Default: `true`.
skills.extra_registries[]kindstringdefault "git"
Registry protocol. Only `"git"` is supported today; other protocols are reserved for a future additive release.
skills.extra_registries[]namestring
Short alias used in `registry:<name>/<skill>` install specs.
skills.extra_registries[]urlstring
Git repository URL of the registry (must expose a top-level `skills/` dir).
skills.install_suggestionsobject
Prompt-triggered skill install suggestions (`[skills.install_suggestions]` section).
skills.install_suggestions.enabledbooleandefault false
Enable suggestions for installable skills before normal agent turns. Default: `false`.
skills.open_skills_dirstring / null
Optional path to a local open-skills repository. If unset, defaults to `$HOME/open-skills` when enabled.
skills.open_skills_enabledbooleandefault false
Enable loading and syncing the community open-skills repository. Default: `false` (opt-in).
skills.prompt_injection_modeoneOf
Skills loading configuration (`[skills]` section).
skills.registry_urlstring / null
URL of the skills registry repository for bare-name installs. Default: `https://github.com/zeroclaw-labs/zeroclaw-skills`
skills.skill_creationobject
Autonomous skill creation configuration (`[skills.skill_creation]` section).
skills.skill_creation.enabledbooleandefault false
Enable automatic skill creation after successful multi-step tasks. Default: `false`.
skills.skill_creation.max_final_answer_charsintegerdefault 2000
Maximum characters of the final assistant answer fed into the reflection prompt. Bounds prompt size so a long answer cannot blow up the reflection request. Default: `2000`.
skills.skill_creation.max_skillsintegerdefault 500
Maximum number of auto-generated skills to keep. When exceeded, the oldest auto-generated skill is removed (LRU eviction).
skills.skill_creation.max_task_charsintegerdefault 1000
Maximum characters of the task description fed into the reflection prompt. Default: `1000`.
skills.skill_creation.max_tool_trace_charsintegerdefault 4000
Maximum characters of the rendered tool-call trace fed into the reflection prompt. Default: `4000`.
skills.skill_creation.reflection_enabledbooleandefault false
Synthesize a canonical `SKILL.md` from the execution trace via a bounded model-provider reflection call instead of the deterministic `SKILL.toml` generator. Requires `enabled = true`. Falls back to `SKILL.toml` whenever the reflection call or its output is invalid, so turning this on never leaves a skill un-created. This is distinct from the `[skills.skill_improvement]` background review fork, which patches existing skills after use rather than creating new ones from a trace. Default: `false`.
skills.skill_creation.similarity_thresholdnumberdefault 0.85
Embedding similarity threshold for deduplication. Skills with descriptions more similar than this value are skipped.
skills.skill_improvementobject
Skill self-improvement configuration (`[skills.skill-improvement]` section). Controls the post-turn background review fork that may patch, expand, or archive skills based on what the conversation revealed. The fork runs in a restricted toolset (only `skills_list`, `skill_view`, `skill_manage`) and never touches the user-visible conversation.
skills.skill_improvement.cooldown_secsintegerdefault 3600
Minimum interval (in seconds) between reviews for the same skill. Acts as a durable rate limit on patches to any one skill. Default: `3600`.
skills.skill_improvement.enabledbooleandefault false
Enable the background skill-review fork. Default: `false`. Opt-in: users must explicitly enable this to allow post-turn skill mutations.
skills.skill_improvement.max_review_iterationsintegerdefault 8
Maximum tool-call iterations the review fork itself is allowed to make. Caps the fork's LLM cost. Default: `8`.
skills.skill_improvement.nudge_interval_iterationsintegerdefault 10
Spawn a review fork once at least this many tool-call iterations have accumulated in the current run. `0` disables iteration-based triggering. Default: `10`.

Skill bundles

Bundled skill sets.

Named skill bundles (`[skill_bundles.<alias>]`).

A map: one entry per alias. The properties below are the shape of a single entry.

propertytype / defaultdescription
skill_bundles.<name>.directorystring / null
Directory path (relative to workspace root) to load skills from.
skill_bundles.<name>.excludearraydefault []
Skill names to exclude from this bundle.
skill_bundles.<name>.includearraydefault []
Skill names to include. Empty means include all skills in `directory`.

Plugins

Plugin processes and the capabilities they hold.

Plugin system configuration (`[plugins]`).

propertytype / defaultdescription
plugins.auto_discoverbooleandefault false
Auto-discover and load plugins on startup (default: false) This gates the package-bound *tool* and *skill* instances the activation plan discovers from installed manifests. Explicit `[channels.plugin.<alias>]` declarations are operator-named, not discovered, so they activate without it.
plugins.enabledbooleandefault false
Enable the plugin system (default: false)
plugins.entriesarraydefault []
plugins.entries[]configobjectdefault {}
plugins.entries[]egress_allow_privatearraydefault []
Subset of `egress_hosts` additionally permitted to resolve to a private, loopback, or link-local address (a self-hosted Gitea, a LAN Nextcloud). Mirrors the `allowed_private_hosts` semantics the host tools already use. This relaxes the *address class* only. It never widens `egress_hosts` — a host listed here but not there is still denied — and cloud metadata addresses stay refused regardless of what is listed, including through a configured `security.nat64_prefixes` translation. Unlike the tool-layer field, `*` is not accepted here.
plugins.entries[]egress_hostsarraydefault []
Destinations this plugin instance may reach. Default: empty, which means **no network reach at all** — a transport permission such as `http_client` grants the surface, this field grants the destinations. Entries are exact hosts (`api.example.com`, `10.0.0.5`) or explicit suffix patterns (`*.example.com`, which matches subdomains but **not** the apex — list the apex separately if it is needed). A bare domain never implies its subdomains, and there is no `*` meaning "anywhere". Ports are not part of an entry: granting a host grants every port on it. Deliberately a **plaintext sibling** of the `#[secret]` `config` map and never inside it: the allowlist is the thing an operator audits, so it has to stay readable in the file they are auditing.
plugins.entries[]namestringdefault ""
plugins.entries[]tls_profilesarray
Named TLS trust and optional client-certificate profiles a transport may select for a destination. A profile chooses certificates only: its `hosts` must each be granted by `egress_hosts`, and selecting it never reaches a destination the grant does not.
plugins.entries[]tls_profiles[]client_certificate_secretstring / null
Secret property holding a PEM client certificate chain (mTLS).
plugins.entries[]tls_profiles[]client_private_key_secretstring / null
Secret property holding the matching PEM client private key (mTLS).
plugins.entries[]tls_profiles[]custom_ca_secretstring / null
Secret property holding one or more PEM CA certificates to trust.
plugins.entries[]tls_profiles[]hostsarraydefault []
Destinations this profile may be used for. Each must be granted by the entry's `egress_hosts`; same grammar.
plugins.entries[]tls_profiles[]namestringdefault ""
Lowercase profile slug a plugin transport selects.
plugins.entries[]tls_profiles[]system_rootsbooleandefault true
Trust the roots plugin HTTPS already trusts (bundled plus this machine's store). Default `true`.
plugins.limitsobject
Per-call WASM execution limits (`[plugins.limits]`). Bounds a single plugin call so a runaway or malicious component traps instead of hanging the host or exhausting memory. `call_fuel` caps instructions per call; `call_timeout_ms` caps elapsed wall-clock time, including time awaiting async host imports; the memory, table, and instance ceilings bound a store's growth. Every value is operator-tunable and validated as non-zero.
plugins.limits.call_fuelintegerdefault 1000000000
Fuel budget per plugin call (wasmtime instruction units).
plugins.limits.call_timeout_msintegerdefault 30000
Wall-clock deadline for one plugin export call, in milliseconds.
plugins.limits.max_connections_per_instanceintegerdefault 16
Maximum live host-owned network connections per logical plugin instance, shared across every transport and every store belonging to it. Unlike the ceilings above this one spans calls rather than bounding a single call, because a connection outlives the call that opened it.
plugins.limits.max_instancesintegerdefault 64
Maximum component instances a plugin store may create.
plugins.limits.max_memory_mbintegerdefault 256
Maximum linear memory a plugin store may grow to, in megabytes.
plugins.limits.max_table_elementsintegerdefault 100000
Maximum table elements a plugin store may allocate.
plugins.max_active_instancesintegerdefault 50
Maximum number of logical plugin instances admitted across capabilities. This counts admitted *instances*, not installed packages: one package that provides both a channel binding and a tool consumes two. It replaces the never-enforced `plugins.max_plugins` key, which counted packages; because the units differ, an old `max_plugins` value is not carried over. See the plugin activation docs for the migration note.
plugins.plugins_dirstringdefault "/Users/tarunrana/.enact/agent/plugins"
Directory where plugins are stored
plugins.securityobject
Plugin signature verification configuration (`[plugins.security]`). Controls Ed25519 signature verification for plugin manifests. In `strict` mode, only plugins signed by a trusted publisher key are loaded. In `permissive` mode, unsigned or untrusted plugins produce warnings but are still loaded. In `disabled` mode (the default), no signature checking occurs.
plugins.security.signature_modestringdefault "disabled"
Signature enforcement mode: "disabled", "permissive", or "strict".
plugins.security.trusted_publisher_keysarraydefault []
Hex-encoded Ed25519 public keys of trusted plugin publishers.

MCP servers

Connected MCP servers and the tools they expose.

External MCP server connections (`[mcp]`).

propertytype / defaultdescription
mcp.deferred_loadingbooleandefault false
Load MCP tool schemas on-demand via `tool_search` instead of eagerly including them in the LLM context window. When enabled, only tool names are listed in the system prompt; the LLM must call `tool_search` to fetch full schemas before invoking a deferred tool.
mcp.enabledbooleandefault true
Enable MCP tool loading.
mcp.serversarraydefault []
Configured MCP servers. The `#[nested]` annotation makes the macro expose this as a List section in `map_key_sections()`, so the dashboard's `+ Add MCP server` affordance and the `POST /api/config/map-key?path=mcp.servers&key=<name>` endpoint pick it up automatically (no hand-table on the gateway side). `#[natural_key = "name"]` opts the Vec into per-element property routing (see `route_vec_path` and the `Configurable` derive's `#[natural_key]` arm). With it, `set_prop("mcp.servers.<name>.url", ...)` and `get_prop("mcp.servers.<name>.transport")` resolve to the matching element's own `set_prop` / `get_prop`, and the `mcp.servers` section behaves like a `HashMap<String, McpServerConfig>` from the dashboard / TUI's point of view. `name` itself becomes read-only via `set_prop`; rename goes through `rename_map_key` which mutates the `name` field in place.
mcp.servers[]argsarraydefault []
Command arguments for stdio transport.
mcp.servers[]commandstringdefault ""
Executable to spawn for stdio transport.
mcp.servers[]envobjectdefault {}
Optional environment variables for stdio transport.
mcp.servers[]headersobjectdefault {}
Optional HTTP headers for HTTP/SSE transports. Treated as secret: the values commonly carry Bearer tokens for the upstream MCP server.
mcp.servers[]max_response_bytesinteger / null
Maximum bytes accepted for a single HTTP/SSE JSON-RPC response body, enforced at read time before the body is parsed or any embedded resource is materialized. A compromised or misbehaving server cannot force an unbounded response-body allocation. This is the *encoded wire* cap, not the decoded materialization budget: `None`/`0` uses the built-in default of 16,078,168 bytes, sized so a full 10 MiB decoded embedded-resource blob (its base64 expansion plus JSON-RPC envelope headroom) still fits. The separate 10 MiB figure is the decoded aggregate blob budget applied after parsing, not this wire cap.
mcp.servers[]namestringdefault ""
Display name used as a tool prefix (`<server>__<tool>`). Filled in from the supplied `map_key` when the entry is created via `create_map_key("mcp.servers", "<name>")`; `#[serde(default)]` lets the macro default-construct from `{}` before the name gets injected.
mcp.servers[]pinned_resourcesarraydefault []
Resource URIs to read once at agent startup and inject into the system prompt as untrusted, server-origin context. Each is read via `resources/read` on this server; pins on a server that does not advertise resources, or that the agent's tool policy denies, are skipped with a warning. Read once per run (not refreshed; no subscriptions).
mcp.servers[]tls_ca_cert_pathstring / null
Absolute path to a PEM-encoded CA certificate or bundle to trust in addition to the default roots for this server's HTTP/SSE transport. Certificate and hostname verification remain enabled. The path must name a regular file no larger than 1 MiB; a missing, unreadable, empty, oversized, non-regular, or invalid file is a hard connection error rather than a fallback to the default trust store. When set, the configured URL and any advertised SSE message endpoint must use HTTPS; plaintext URLs and downgrade redirects are rejected. Ignored by stdio.
mcp.servers[]tool_timeout_secsinteger / null
Optional per-call timeout in seconds (hard capped in validation).
mcp.servers[]transportoneOf
Transport type for MCP server connections.
mcp.servers[]urlstring / null
URL for HTTP/SSE transports.

MCP bundles

Deferred MCP servers, loaded on demand.

Named MCP server bundles (`[mcp_bundles.<alias>]`).

A map: one entry per alias. The properties below are the shape of a single entry.

propertytype / defaultdescription
mcp_bundles.<name>.excludearraydefault []
MCP server IDs removed from the grant. Deny wins: a name listed here is excluded even if another referenced bundle includes it.
mcp_bundles.<name>.serversarraydefault []
MCP server IDs (`[mcp.servers].name`) granted by this bundle.

Memory

Long-term memory policy.

Memory backend configuration: sqlite, markdown, embeddings (`[memory]`).

propertytype / defaultdescription
memory.archive_after_daysintegerdefault 7
Move daily/session files to the archive directory after this many days. Keeps the hot working set small without deleting history.
memory.audit_enabledbooleandefault false
Enable audit logging of memory operations.
memory.audit_retention_daysintegerdefault 30
Retention period for audit entries in days (default: 30).
memory.auto_hydratebooleandefault true
Auto-hydrate from MEMORY_SNAPSHOT.md when brain.db is missing
memory.auto_reindex_on_identity_changebooleandefault false
Automatically re-embed all memories in the background when a change of embedding provider/model/dimensions is detected at startup (after the stale vectors have been cleared). Costs one embedding API call per memory, so it's off by default — leave it off for large stores and run `zeroclaw memory reindex` explicitly instead.
memory.auto_savebooleandefault true
Auto-save what *you* tell ZeroClaw into memory as conversation history — the agent's own replies are not saved. Turn off if you want memory to only hold things you explicitly record via the memory tool.
memory.backendstringdefault "sqlite"
Dotted reference to the active storage instance: `<backend>.<alias>` (e.g. `"sqlite.default"`, `"postgres.work"`). Resolves through `Config.storage.<backend>.<alias>` at runtime. Bare backend names (`"sqlite"`) are treated as `"<backend>.default"`. Set to `"none"` to disable persistence entirely.
memory.candidate_multiplierintegerdefault 4
Candidate pool multiplier over the final recall limit before blend/rerank trimming. Values must be in 1..=20; runtime also enforces a bounded candidate pool.
memory.chunk_max_tokensintegerdefault 512
Max tokens per chunk for document splitting
memory.conflict_supersede_enabledbooleandefault true
Enable reversible supersede soft-hide machinery when wired.
memory.conflict_thresholdnumberdefault 0.85
Cosine similarity threshold for conflict detection (0.0–1.0).
memory.consolidation_extract_factsbooleandefault false
Also extract atomic durable facts from each consolidated turn and store them as individual Core memories. Default off; the flip is sequenced in a later phase. SQLite-only: enabling this requires the sqlite memory backend globally and on every agent (validated at config load).
memory.conversation_retention_daysintegerdefault 30
Delete conversation rows older than this many days from the DB (sqlite backend only). Age is measured by `updated_at` (last write time). 0 = keep forever.
memory.core_max_bytesintegerdefault 0
Maximum Core bytes before budget compaction. 0 = unbounded.
memory.core_max_rowsintegerdefault 0
Maximum Core rows before budget compaction. 0 = unbounded.
memory.core_retention_daysintegerdefault 0
Delete core memory rows older than this many days from the DB. Age is measured by `created_at` (first-write time). Neither recall nor ordinary rewrites refresh `created_at` under the current SQLite upsert, so core retention is an absolute age limit from first write. Set this to a generously large window for durable core memories, or keep 0 = keep forever.
memory.daily_max_rowsintegerdefault 0
Maximum Daily rows before budget compaction. 0 = unbounded.
memory.daily_retention_daysintegerdefault 0
Delete daily memory rows older than this many days from the DB. Age is measured by `updated_at` (last write time). 0 = keep forever.
memory.dedup_actiononeOf
Write-time duplicate handling policy for memory entries.
memory.dedup_jaccard_thresholdnumberdefault 0.8
Jaccard threshold for text-only duplicate detection.
memory.dedup_on_writebooleandefault false
Enable write-time near-duplicate detection.
memory.default_namespacestringdefault "default"
Default namespace for memory entries.
memory.embedding_api_keystring / null
Optional API key for the embedding endpoint. When set, embedding calls use this key instead of inheriting one from the seed model provider — decoupling embeddings from the chat model. Use it when the chat model runs on a provider that carries no usable embedding credential (e.g. an OAuth-only provider) while embeddings keep hitting an `openai`/`custom:` endpoint with their own key. Leave unset to inherit the seed provider's key (backward-compatible default).
memory.embedding_cache_sizeintegerdefault 10000
Max embedding cache entries before LRU eviction
memory.embedding_dimensionsintegerdefault 1536
Vector width produced by the embedding model — must match the model's native dimension or vectors won't store correctly. Look up the number on the model_provider's model page.
memory.embedding_modelstringdefault "text-embedding-3-small"
Embedding model identifier — must match a model your chosen embedding model_provider serves (e.g. `text-embedding-3-small` for OpenAI). Changing this invalidates existing embeddings: the change is detected at startup and stale vectors are cleared automatically; run `zeroclaw memory reindex` to re-embed (or set `auto_reindex_on_identity_change`).
memory.embedding_providerstringdefault "none"
Source of embedding vectors for semantic search. `none` = keyword-only retrieval (no API calls, no vector cost); `openai` = OpenAI's embedding API; `custom:URL` = any OpenAI-compatible embedding endpoint (LiteLLM, local gateway, etc.).
memory.evict_orderoneOf
Memory budget eviction order.
memory.fts_early_return_scorenumberdefault 0.85
Reserved (0.0-1.0): the FTS score above which recall would skip the vector stage. Inert until the backend exposes distinct FTS and vector operations; recall is a single hybrid call today, so this has no effect.
memory.hygiene_enabledbooleandefault true
Run the periodic hygiene pass that archives stale daily/session files and enforces retention windows. Leave on unless you want to manage cleanup yourself.
memory.importance_weightnumberdefault 0.2
Importance weight used by the recall blend.
memory.keyword_weightnumberdefault 0.3
How heavily BM25 (keyword) overlap counts when `search_mode = hybrid`. Raise toward 1.0 for exact-term matching; lower it when paraphrases should still score well.
memory.min_relevance_scorenumberdefault 0.4
Minimum hybrid score (0.0–1.0) for a memory to be included in context. Memories scoring below this threshold are dropped to prevent irrelevant context from bleeding into conversations. Default: 0.4
memory.mmr_lambdanumberdefault 0.7
MMR relevance-vs-diversity weight, where 1.0 means relevance-only.
memory.pin_min_importancenumberdefault 1.01
Pin entries at or above this importance. >1.0 means disabled.
memory.pin_namespacesarraydefault []
Namespaces protected from budget eviction.
memory.policyobject

memory.policy.max_entries_per_categoryintegerdefault 0
Maximum entries per category (0 = unlimited).

Maximum entries per namespace (0 = unlimited).

memory.policy.redact_categoriesarraydefault ["secret", "api_key", "private_key", "email", "phone"]

memory.purge_after_daysintegerdefault 30
Delete archived files permanently after this many days. Set high if you need long-term history; set low for privacy / disk-space reasons.
memory.recall_date_stampbooleandefault true

memory.recency_weightnumberdefault 0.1

memory.rerank_strategystringdefault "none"
Advanced rerank strategy. Valid: "none", "mmr".
memory.rerank_thresholdintegerdefault 5
Minimum candidate count to trigger the advanced rerank strategy.
memory.response_cache_enabledbooleandefault false
Enable LLM response caching to avoid paying for duplicate prompts

Max in-memory hot cache entries for the two-tier response cache (default: 256)

Max number of cached responses before LRU eviction (default: 5000)
memory.response_cache_ttl_minutesintegerdefault 60
TTL in minutes for cached responses (default: 60)
memory.retrieval_stagesarraydefault ["fts", "vector"]
Retrieval stages for per-agent recall. Only `"cache"` is active: it enables an in-process, per-handle hot cache over recall results and is omitted from the default so recall stays coherent across handles. `"fts"` and `"vector"` are reserved for when the backend exposes distinct FTS and vector operations; recall is a single hybrid backend call today, so those names have no effect (kept for forward compat).
memory.search_modeoneOf

Standard operating procedures

SOP execution and approval gates.

Standard Operating Procedures engine configuration (`[sop]`).

propertytype / defaultdescription
sop.approvalobject
`[sop.approval]` - approval broker policy config. A permanent identity source for channel-provided approvers (not a stopgap): the approval broker consumes it for group-membership and quorum checks. Empty = no broker policy applies.
sop.approval.groupsobject
Named approver groups: `group name -> members`. A member is matched against the transport-derived (channel-authenticated) `ApprovalPrincipal` identity. A member may be source-qualified (`<source>:<identity>`, e.g. `http:ZeroClawOperator`, `ws:<subject>`, `agent:<alias>`) to grant rights on one transport only, or a bare identity (`alice`) to grant from any non-channel source. Channel members must include the channel namespace (`channel:<channel-key>:<sender>`) so sender ids from different channel aliases cannot collide. A future auth system adds a second resolver alongside this one; it does not replace channel identities.
sop.approval.policiesobject
Named approval policies a SOP step may reference by name.
sop.approval_modeoneOf
WHO may clear a SOP approval gate. Layered with execution_mode / priority / requires_confirmation (all of which still apply). Closed set => serde enum.
sop.approval_timeout_actiononeOf
What happens to a SOP approval gate when it times out. Default is fail-closed: the gate never self-approves. Closed set => serde enum.
sop.approval_timeout_secsintegerdefault 300
Approval timeout in seconds. When a run waits for approval longer than this, the configured `approval_timeout_action` is applied (default `escalate`: re-surface the gate to the out-of-band approver and never self-approve). Set to 0 to disable the timeout sweep.
sop.default_execution_modestringdefault "supervised"
Default execution mode for SOPs that omit `execution_mode`. Values: `auto`, `supervised` (default), `step_by_step`, `priority_based`, `deterministic`.
sop.maintenance_interval_secsintegerdefault 60
How often (seconds) the daemon runs the SOP maintenance tick: fire fail-closed approval timeouts (per `approval_timeout_secs` / `approval_timeout_action`), reap expired concurrency-claim leases, and prune terminal runs past `max_finished_runs`. Default `60`; set to `0` to disable the tick entirely. The tick itself self-approves nothing - timeout handling is governed by `approval_timeout_action` (default `escalate`).
sop.max_concurrent_totalintegerdefault 4
Maximum total concurrent SOP runs across all SOPs.
sop.max_finished_runsintegerdefault 100
Maximum number of finished runs kept in memory for status queries. Oldest runs are evicted when over capacity. 0 = unlimited.
sop.max_step_retriesintegerdefault 2
Maximum retries allowed by a step failure policy.
sop.max_step_visitsintegerdefault 256
Maximum times a routed SOP run can visit one step.
sop.persist_runsbooleandefault true
Persist run state durably across restarts. Default `true`: `build_sop_engine` selects the configured backend (`sqlite`) and in-flight runs - including runs parked at a HITL approval - survive a restart. This is the durable substrate the HITL admission model relies on so a pending approval is not lost when the daemon restarts. Set to `false` to opt back into ephemeral in-memory state.
sop.procedural_memory_enabledbooleandefault false
Enable SOP procedural-memory proposal tooling. Default false keeps self-modifying SOP write-back opt-in while the SOP subsystem is Experimental.
sop.run_state_dirstring / null
Directory for the durable run store (created mode-0700). When omitted, `<data_dir>/sop`. Never OS-temp.
sop.run_store_backendoneOf
Durable SOP run-state backend selector. A closed, compile-time-known set, so it is a serde enum rather than free text (mirrors `SandboxBackend` / `ObservabilityBackend` in this file); unknown values are rejected at parse time.
sop.sops_dirstring / null
Directory containing SOP definitions (subdirs with SOP.toml + SOP.md). A relative value resolves against the install root (matching the `skill-bundles` convention), so the documented `shared/sops` loads from `<install>/shared/sops` — the same directory the web/RPC SOP author writes to. An absolute or `~`-prefixed value is used as-is. Unset by default; SOP runtime behavior activates only when this is set to a non-empty value, so leaving it unset (or an explicit empty value) keeps SOP loading disabled (unset/empty still falls back to `<install>/shared/sops` for offline CLI inspection).
sop.step_mandatory_toolsarraydefault ["sop_advance", "sop_approve", "sop_status"]
Tool names that remain available while step scope is enforced.
sop.step_schema_enforcebooleandefault true
Enforce per-step input/output schemas when a step declares them.
sop.step_scope_enforcebooleandefault false
Enforce per-step tool scope. Default false keeps `tools:` advisory.
sop.untrusted_frame_warningbooleandefault true
Include the explanatory warning text inside untrusted-content frames. Boundary framing itself is always on once wired.
sop.untrusted_guard_sensitivitynumberdefault 0.7
Prompt-guard and outbound-redaction sensitivity for untrusted SOP content.
sop.untrusted_input_guardstringdefault "warn"
Prompt-guard action for untrusted SOP trigger input: warn, block, or sanitize.
sop.untrusted_outbound_redactbooleandefault true
Redact outbound SOP content before persistence/audit consumers write it. Intended to converge with the shared B/F redaction switch once those consumers land.
sop.untrusted_payload_max_bytesintegerdefault 8192
Maximum bytes accepted from untrusted SOP trigger payload/topic content before char-boundary truncation. 0 disables the cap.

Permission profiles

What each profile allows.

Named permission profiles (`[permission_profiles.<alias>]`): the single runtime authorization vocabulary. OIDC claim mappings and user roster entries resolve here; deny-by-default: anything a profile does not grant is refused.

A map: one entry per alias. The properties below are the shape of a single entry.

propertytype / defaultdescription
permission_profiles.<name>.adminbooleandefault false
Grant everything. When true all other fields are irrelevant.
permission_profiles.<name>.allowed_agentsarraydefault []
Agent aliases holders of this profile may bind or address. Empty grants NO agents; grant every agent with the explicit `"*"` entry.
permission_profiles.<name>.allowed_toolsarraydefault []
Tool names holders may cause an agent to run. Empty grants NO tools; broad access requires the explicit `"*"` entry. This selector composes with the coarse `tools = ["execute"]` grant, never replaces it: without that grant the holder's sessions run tool-less whatever is named here. (Note this differs from risk-profile `allowed_tools`, where empty means unconstrained: permission profiles are deny-by-default. The agent's own risk-profile policy still applies on top.)
permission_profiles.<name>.config_write_pathsarraydefault []
Dotted config path prefixes holders may write. A trailing `.*` grants the subtree (e.g. `channels.*`); a bare path grants that exact prop; `"*"` grants every path. Empty grants NO paths.
permission_profiles.<name>.grantsobjectdefault {}
Resource-class grants: for each resource kind, the verbs permitted. Resources: `system`, `sessions`, `memory`, `cron`, `config`, `agents`, `cost`, `skills`, `personality`, `logs`, `tui`, `files`, `locales`, `quickstart`, `channels`, `providers`, `models`, `peer_groups`, `plugins`, `tools`, `sops`. Verbs: `create`, `read`, `update`, `delete`, `execute`. An unlisted resource is denied.

Risk profiles

The risk tiers the permission profiles grant.

Named risk/autonomy profiles (`[risk_profiles.<alias>]`).

A map: one entry per alias. The properties below are the shape of a single entry.

propertytype / defaultdescription
risk_profiles.<name>.allowed_commandsarraydefault ["git", "npm", "cargo", "ls", "cat", "grep", "find", "echo", "pwd", "wc", "head", "tail", "date", "df", "du", "uname", "uptime", "hostname", "python", "python3", "pip", "node"]
Allowlist of executable names for shell execution.
risk_profiles.<name>.allowed_rootsarraydefault []
Extra directory roots the agent may access.
risk_profiles.<name>.allowed_toolsarraydefault []
Tools the agent may call in agentic mode. An omitted field and an explicit `allowed_tools = []` are the same legacy state: no authorization constraint (unrestricted). A non-empty list is an explicit closed set for built-ins and MCP; skill tools remain registered unless listed in `excluded_tools`. For an explicit deny-all gate, set [`Self::deny_all_tools`] — an empty list does NOT mean deny-all. MCP exception: when the list is non-empty, runtime-discovered MCP tools (any name containing `__`, which is the `<server>__<tool>` convention used by the MCP wrapper) are auto-admitted into the effective allow-list without needing to be listed here individually. This keeps the post-change eager-MCP default usable for agents with an explicit allow-list. Block individual MCP tools via `excluded_tools`. Scope of the exception: the `__` auto-admit applies only to this risk-profile allow-list, **not** to caller-supplied per-run `allowed_tools` (cron job `allowed_tools`, narrowed delegate invocations, etc.). Per-run lists are still strict explicit-list intersections, so a job that narrows `allowed_tools = ["cron_add"]` will not see runtime-discovered MCP tools unless it names them.
risk_profiles.<name>.always_askarraydefault []
Tools that always require approval in this profile.
risk_profiles.<name>.approval_routeanyOf
Route this profile's tool approvals to a DISTINCT approver channel instead of the channel that triggered the run (closes the cross-channel-HITL gap). Absent ⇒ the originating channel approves (today's behavior). See [`crate::autonomy::ApprovalRoute`]. Honored on both the interactive channel-driven path and the non-interactive turn path (gateway chat/webhook dispatch and agent-to-agent peer messages). On the non-interactive path the approver is resolved from the live daemon channel registry; if no registry is available or the approver is not live, the gate keeps the non-interactive default (fail-closed deny under the default `on_no_approver`).
risk_profiles.<name>.auto_approvearraydefault ["file_read", "memory_recall", "web_search_tool", "web_fetch", "calculator", "glob_search", "content_search", "image_info", "weather", "tool_search", "browser_open"]
Tools that never require approval in this profile.
risk_profiles.<name>.block_high_risk_commandsbooleandefault true
Block high-risk commands even when allowlisted.
risk_profiles.<name>.delegation_policyobject
Risk-profile delegation policy for work sent to agents that share it.
risk_profiles.<name>.delegation_policy.modeoneOf
Delegation mode for a risk profile.
risk_profiles.<name>.deny_all_toolsboolean
Explicit deny-all for this profile: no tool may be invoked under it (built-ins, MCP tools, and skill-defined tools alike — there is no `__` auto-admit under deny-all). `allowed_tools = []` remains legacy-unrestricted; setting both `deny_all_tools = true` and a non-empty `allowed_tools` is a configuration error rejected by [`Config::validate`].
risk_profiles.<name>.excluded_toolsarraydefault []
Tools excluded from non-CLI channels under this profile. Also subtracts from the agentic-delegate allow-list resolved at runtime, which is the only way to block individual `<server>__<tool>` MCP names that would otherwise be auto-admitted by the `allowed_tools` MCP exception described above.
risk_profiles.<name>.firejail_argsarraydefault []
Extra arguments forwarded to firejail when sandbox_backend = "firejail".
risk_profiles.<name>.forbidden_pathsarraydefault ["/etc", "/root", "/home", "/usr", "/bin", "/sbin", "/lib", "/opt", "/boot", "/dev", "/proc", "/sys", "/var", "/tmp", "~/.ssh", "~/.gnupg", "~/.aws", "~/.config"]
Explicit path denylist.
risk_profiles.<name>.leveloneOf
The agent's autonomy level, ordered from least to most autonomous.
risk_profiles.<name>.require_approval_for_medium_riskbooleandefault true
Require approval for medium-risk operations.
risk_profiles.<name>.sandbox_backendstring / null
Sandbox backend identifier (e.g. `"firejail"`, `"landlock"`). `None` inherits.
risk_profiles.<name>.sandbox_enabledboolean / null
Whether the sandbox is enabled for this profile. `None` inherits global.
risk_profiles.<name>.sandbox_imagestring / null
Container image the docker sandbox runs commands in when `sandbox_backend = "docker"`. `None` inherits the built-in default. Set this to pin a digest or a specific tag so the sandbox stops tracking whatever the default tag moves to.

Environment variable names passed through to shell subprocesses.

Restrict filesystem access to workspace-relative paths. Default: `false`.

Agents

Per-agent overrides, including the tools each may use.

Aliased agents in this install. Each entry under `[agents.<alias>]` is one user-facing agent with its own identity, channels, model provider, risk profile, workspace, and memory scope. `DelegateTool` consults this map when one agent delegates a subtask to another.

A map: one entry per alias. The properties below are the shape of a single entry.

propertytype / defaultdescription
agents.<name>.a2aobject
Per-agent A2A publication and exposed-skill configuration.
agents.<name>.a2a.exposed_skillsarraydefault []
Filter selecting which resolved skill ids appear on this alias's card. Empty = no skills advertised. Entries that do not resolve to a real skill in the alias's bundles are dropped (the bundles are canonical; this only selects from them).
agents.<name>.a2a.publishedbooleandefault false
Publish this alias as a discoverable A2A agent. Default `false`: the alias is excluded from the discovery catalog and serves no per-alias card even when the server is enabled.
agents.<name>.acp_enable_mcpbooleandefault false
Initialize this agent's `mcp_bundles` tools when it serves an ACP (`session/new`) session. Off by default: MCP servers are external processes/services that can block startup while they connect, and ACP `session/new` is expected to return promptly. Enable it when this agent must call its `mcp_bundles` tools over ACP; `session/new` then pays the one-time MCP connection cost (bounded and non-fatal per server). Set per agent so each ACP profile opts in independently; when this agent is the ACP default (`acp.default_agent`, or the sole configured agent), the flag is picked up automatically for sessions that omit `agentAlias`.

agents.<name>.cron_jobsarraydefault []
Cron job aliases. Each entry references `cron[key]`, a declarative scheduled job invoked by the scheduler on its configured trigger. When the cron fires, this agent is the actor that executes the job. Exactly one enabled agent may claim a given id: a job listed by two enabled agents is refused rather than run under an arbitrary one, unless its row already carries a stored owner from before the second claim was added.

Security

The security posture the runtime enforces.

Security subsystem configuration (`[security]`).

propertytype / defaultdescription
security.auditobject
Audit logging configuration **Scope:** the audit trail currently records certificate issuance and renewal. Command execution is NOT audited: no runtime path calls `AuditLogger::log_command_event` outside tests, so no tool command, its arguments, its approval or its rejection is ever written here. Treat this section as the certificate trail, not as a record of what the agent ran.
security.audit.enabledbooleandefault true
Enable audit logging. Defaults to `true`, which is what keeps the certificate issuance and renewal trail being written. Setting it to `false` turns that trail off: certificate issuance and renewal then have no audit-log record. Enabling it does not start recording command execution, which has no production writer.
security.audit.log_pathstringdefault "audit.log"
Path to audit log file (relative to zeroclaw dir). Receives the certificate issuance and renewal events. No command execution record is ever written to it.
security.audit.max_size_mbintegerdefault 100
Maximum log size in MB before rotation. Applies to the certificate trail written at `log_path`.
security.audit.sign_eventsbooleandefault false
Sign events with HMAC for tamper evidence. Applies to the certificate trail written at `log_path`. It cannot make command execution tamper-evident, because command execution is not recorded.
security.estopobject
Emergency stop configuration.
security.estop.enabledbooleandefault false
Enable emergency stop controls.
security.estop.require_otp_to_resumebooleandefault true
Require a valid OTP before resume operations.
security.estop.state_filestringdefault "/Users/tarunrana/.enact/agent/estop-state.json"
File path used to persist estop state.
security.leak_detectionobject
Outbound credential leak detection configuration. These settings control the final guardrail pass over outbound channel responses before they are delivered. Deterministic credential patterns include API keys, private keys, database URLs, bot tokens, and related token syntax. The high-entropy pass is a separate heuristic for standalone opaque tokens.
security.leak_detection.enabledbooleandefault true
Enable outbound credential leak detection and redaction.
security.leak_detection.high_entropy_tokensbooleandefault true
Enable high-entropy token redaction; deterministic patterns still run when false.
security.leak_detection.sensitivitynumberdefault 0.7
Detection sensitivity from 0.0 to 1.0; higher is more aggressive.
security.nat64_prefixesarraydefault []
Network-specific RFC 6052 NAT64 prefixes deployed on this host's network, for example `[security] nat64_prefixes = ["2001:db8:122:344::/96"]`. Default: `[]`. A NAT64 translator delivers any IPv6 destination inside one of these prefixes to the IPv4 address embedded in it. The prefix is an organization's own choice and nothing in the address reveals it, so without this list an attacker-controlled hostname can resolve to an apparently-global IPv6 address that the local translator maps to `10.0.0.1` or `169.254.169.254`, and the outbound SSRF checks accept it. Declaring the prefixes in use makes the address they translate to part of the validation boundary for `http_request`, `web_fetch`, and `text_browser`. Each entry is `<ipv6>/<length>` with one of RFC 6052 §2.2's lengths — 32, 40, 48, 56, 64, or 96 — and no bits set beyond that length. A malformed entry fails construction of the tools that read this list rather than being skipped, so a typo cannot silently narrow the boundary. The default is correct for deployments that run no NAT64 translator and for those that use only the well-known `64:ff9b::/96` prefix, which is classified without configuration. RFC 8215 also standardizes `64:ff9b:1::/48` as the local-use NAT64 block, and it still needs declaring. The public-address validator already denies the whole block as non-global, so the default is safe on its own. But that non-global check is precisely what the private-host opt-in relaxes, and the metadata gate that remains unconditional underneath it decodes only the well-known `/96`, not this block. A deployment that translates from `64:ff9b:1::/48` must therefore declare the specific prefix it uses here; otherwise, once a host is allowed through `allowed_private_hosts`, an address in that block reaches the translator's embedded IPv4 destination without that destination being classified. Declared prefixes may overlap, and an address inside several of them decodes to a *different* IPv4 destination under each. Validation is conservative: an address is accepted only when every declared translation it matches lands somewhere acceptable, so a nested prefix that decodes an address to a private or metadata destination denies it even when a broader prefix decodes the same address globally. If that refuses destinations you consider legitimate, narrow the declared prefixes to the translations actually deployed.
security.otpobject
Security OTP configuration.
security.otp.cache_valid_secsintegerdefault 300
Reuse window for recently validated OTP codes.
security.otp.challenge_max_attemptsintegerdefault 3
Maximum number of OTP challenge attempts before lockout.
security.otp.enabledbooleandefault false
Enable OTP gating. Defaults to disabled for backward compatibility.
security.otp.gated_actionsarraydefault ["shell", "file_write", "browser_open", "browser", "memory_forget"]
Tool/action names gated by OTP. Empty or malformed entries are rejected at config load; an entry that does not match a known gated action is accepted but logged at WARN, since it cannot be enforced.
security.otp.gated_domain_categoriesarraydefault []
Domain-category presets expanded into `gated_domains`.
security.otp.gated_domainsarraydefault []
Explicit domain patterns gated by OTP.
security.otp.methodoneOf
OTP validation strategy.
security.otp.token_ttl_secsintegerdefault 30
TOTP time-step in seconds.
security.trust_daemon_uidbooleandefault true
Whether the daemon's OWN uid keeps the trusted shared-operator path on the local socket even when a `[users]` roster is configured. Default `true`: the operator who runs the daemon (and owns its config file) retains local authority, which is also what makes local-only lockout recovery possible. Set `false` to require every local peer — including the daemon's own uid — to map through `[users.<name>].uid` or present a credential.
security.webauthnobject
WebAuthn / FIDO2 hardware key authentication configuration (`[security.webauthn]`). Enables registration and authentication via hardware security keys (YubiKey, SoloKey, etc.) and platform authenticators (Touch ID, Windows Hello).
security.webauthn.enabledbooleandefault false
Enable WebAuthn authentication. Default: false.
security.webauthn.rp_idstringdefault "localhost"
Relying Party ID (domain name, e.g. "example.com"). Default: "localhost".
security.webauthn.rp_namestringdefault "ZeroClaw"
Relying Party display name. Default: "ZeroClaw".
security.webauthn.rp_originstringdefault "http://localhost:42617"
Relying Party origin URL (e.g. `"https://example.com"`). Default: `"http://localhost:42617"`.

Trust

How this was measured

Every schema is real

Schemas are read out of each tool’s parameters_schema() — the json! block in the Rust source — and resolved against the constants, associated constants, message catalogue and table folds that block refers to. Where a value genuinely cannot be known from the source, it is marked <computed at runtime> rather than guessed.

Cross-checked against the binary

The shipped binary was driven over MCP (mcp serve --expose-full) and its advertised inputSchema captured. 52 tools are exposed that way. Every one of the 109 extracted schemas was compared against them; the only differences are the 1 tools the overlay rewrites, listed above.

Where the numbers come from

Tool names, tiers, compilation modes and operations come from zeroclaw-tools-operations.csv, the operations inventory. The family table comes from overlays/enact/native/tools/families.rs. The tier and compilation table is cross-read from crates/zeroclaw-tools/src/inventory.rs. No count in this report is written by hand — all of them are derived.
